).
∎

F.2
Proof of (
8
) for
p\in(|V|-1,\infty]

In this section, we prove the following upper bound for
|\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}|
for any
p
in
(|V|-1,\infty]
:

\displaystyle|\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p|V|}\cdot\left((p|V|)^{2}\right)^{(p|V|)^{2}}\cdot c^{|E|}\cdot\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\cdot L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right),

(54)

where
c
is a small constant independent of
G
.

Invoking Lemma
4.2
, and summing up
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)
over all possible degree configurations
\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq\min(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}),({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}
, we get (for some small constant
c
that we will pick later)

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)

\displaystyle\leq\underset{\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}2^{p|V|}\quad\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\cdot L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}

(55)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p|V|}\left(\underset{\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}\quad\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}\quad\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

\displaystyle=2^{p|V|}\left(\underset{\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}\quad\cdot\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}\quad\cdot\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p|V|}\left(\underset{\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}\quad\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\quad\left(\underset{\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}}

(56)

\displaystyle=2^{p|V|}\left(\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\quad\underset{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}:d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

(57)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p|V|}\cdot 3^{|E|}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\frac{1}{z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\quad\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\cdot L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{\frac{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}}

(58)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p|V|}\cdot\left((p|V|)^{2}\right)^{(p|V|)^{2}}\cdot 3^{|E|}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\cdot L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}.

(59)

Here, (
56
) follows by a direct application of Hölder’s inequality (assuming the following is true):

\displaystyle\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}\frac{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}\geq 1.

(60)

We prove (
60
) here (assuming
s
is the source vertex in
G
):

\displaystyle\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}\frac{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}=\left(\underset{({s}\rightarrow{w})\in E}{\sum}\frac{x_{({s}\rightarrow{w})}}{p}\right)+\left(\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E\setminus\{({s}\rightarrow{w})\in E\}}{\sum}\frac{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}\right)\geq 1,

where the inequality follows from (
6
) for
s
and the fact that
x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\geq 0
for every
({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E
.x

Further, (
57
) follows by

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq d_{({u}\rightarrow{w})}\leq L_{{({u}\rightarrow{w})}}}{\sum}L_{{({u}\rightarrow{w},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}=L_{({u}\rightarrow{w})}^{p}

and pushing the sum inside on
L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}
for each
d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}
. We prove (
58
) as follows:

\displaystyle\underset{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}:d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}\quad d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}.

Recall our assumption that the
d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}
values are powers of two and
z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}>0
and as a result, we have (assuming
f=\log(L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)})
)

\displaystyle 1^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}+2^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}+2^{2z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}+\dots+2^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\cdot f}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}(f+1)}-1}{2^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\frac{2^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}f}}{2^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}f}}{1-2^{-z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\cdot\frac{2^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}f}}{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)\cdot 3\cdot L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}.

In the above, the first equation follows by treating the left hand side expression as a geometric progression with first term
1
and common difference
2^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}
. The final inequality follows by noting that for small enough
z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}>0
,
1-2^{-z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}
is at least
\frac{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{c}
for any
c\geq 3
.

Finally, we prove (
59
) by invoking a standard result in linear programming, which we state in the language of
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
below. Recall that
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
has rational coefficients for every constraint and each entry in the constraint matrix has only values in
[0,1]
. Consider an optimal basic feasible solution
(\mathbf{x}^{*},\mathbf{z}^{*})=\left(x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)
to
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
on
G
such that for every
({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E
with
z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}>0
and
x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}>0
, we have applying Cramer’s rule, we get
z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\geq\frac{1}{(p(2|E|+|V|))!}\geq\frac{1}{(p|V|^{2})!}\geq\frac{1}{\left((p|V|)^{2}\right)^{(p|V|)^{2}}}
.
30
30

30

We would like to note here that these bounds hold for any basic feasible solution as well. For optimal basic feasible solutions, we can achieve a better bound than this one but we stick to this since it is sufficient for our arguments.

In other words, this implies that the non-zero values of an optimal basic feasible solution of any linear program
with rational coefficients are polynomially bounded in the size of its input. The above theorem immediately gives us (
59
) since

\displaystyle\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\frac{1}{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}

\displaystyle\leq\left((p|V|)^{2}\right)^{(p|V|)^{2}},

as required. Note that this combinatorial result implies the runtime of Algorithm
2.2
as well.
This completes the proof.

F.3
Proof of (
8
) for
p\leq|V|-1

Invoking Lemma
4.2
and summing up
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)
over all possible degree configurations

\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq\min\left\{L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}\right\},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E},

we get

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{d}}{\sum}\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)

\displaystyle\leq\underset{\mathbf{d}}{\sum}2^{p|V|}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\cdot L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

(61)

\displaystyle=2^{p|V|}\underset{\mathbf{d}}{\sum}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\cdot\left(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p|V|}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(\underset{\mathbf{d}}{\sum}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)^{z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\left(\underset{\mathbf{d}}{\sum}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}}

(62)

\displaystyle=2^{p|V|}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(\left(\underset{\mathbf{d}}{\sum}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)^{z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

(63)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p|V|}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(\left(2\cdot L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}\right)^{z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\cdot L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

(64)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{(p+1)|V|}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{v})\in E}{\prod}\left(L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}^{z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\cdot L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right).

(65)

Here, (
62
) follows by a direct application of Hölder’s inequality assuming the following:

31
31

31

The proof for the case when
p>|V|-1
actually diverges at this point and we push the sums differently instead of doing it through (
66
).

\displaystyle\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}\left(z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}+\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}\right)\geq 1.

(66)

We prove (
66
) below:

\displaystyle\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}\left(z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}+\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})}{\sum}\left(\frac{z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p+1}+\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{|V|}{p+1}

\displaystyle\geq 1,

where the first inequality follows from (
7
) and the fact that
p+1>0
. The second inequality follows by summing up (
6
) for every
u\in V
and the final inequality follows from our assumption that
p\leq|V|-1
. To complete the proof, we prove (
63
) and (
64
), where the former follows from

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq d_{({u}\rightarrow{w})}\leq L_{{({u}\rightarrow{w})}}}{\sum}L_{{({u}\rightarrow{w},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}=L_{({u}\rightarrow{w})}^{p}

and the latter follows by definition of
d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}
values being powers of two. This completes the proof.

To complete the proof of Theorem
4.3
, we need to prove (
9
) as well, which we do using the dual of
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
in Appendix
F.4
.

F.4
Proof of (
9
), (
11
) and (
15
)

In this section, we prove a lower bound for
|\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}|
by constructing an instance
I=\{R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}:||R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}||_{p}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},||R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}||_{\infty}\leq L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E\}
using the dual of
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
. We would like to note here that our lower bound holds for any
G
, any
p\in[1,\infty)
and also for the case when there are no
\ell_{\infty}
constraints.

We start by stating the dual of
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
.

\displaystyle\max\quad\underset{u\in V}{\sum}y_{u}

\displaystyle\frac{y_{v}}{p}+y_{u}\leq\log(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})\quad\forall({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E

(67)

\displaystyle y_{u}\leq\log(L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)})\quad\forall u\in V

(68)

\displaystyle y_{u}\geq 0\quad\forall u\in V.

(69)

We will now construct a join instance
\{R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}:({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E\}
based on an optimal dual solution
\mathbf{y}^{*}=(y^{*}_{u})_{u\in V}
such that

\displaystyle||R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}||_{p}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},||R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}||_{\infty}\leq L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)},\quad\forall({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E

and

\displaystyle|\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}|\geq\frac{1}{2^{|V|}}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right),

where recall that
(\mathbf{x}^{*},\mathbf{z}^{*})=(x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}
denotes an optimal solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
.

Given
\mathbf{y}^{*}
, we define
\mathrm{Dom}(u)=\left[{\left\lfloor 2^{y^{*}_{u}}\right\rfloor}\right]
for every
u\in V
and
R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}=\mathrm{Dom}(v)\times\mathrm{Dom}(u)
for every
({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E
. For each
({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E
, we have

\displaystyle||R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}||_{p}

\displaystyle=\sqrt[p]{\underset{\mathrm{val}_{v}\in\mathrm{Dom}(v)}{\sum}|\mathrm{Dom}(u)|^{p}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt[p]{|\mathrm{Dom}(v)|\cdot|\mathrm{Dom}(u)|^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt[p]{2^{y^{*}_{v}}\cdot 2^{p\cdot y^{*}_{u}}}

\displaystyle=2^{\frac{y^{*}_{v}}{p}}2^{y^{*}_{u}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\log(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})}\quad\text{ follows from~\eqref{eq:gen-dual-ub}}

\displaystyle=L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}

and

\displaystyle||R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}||_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\underset{\mathrm{val}_{v}\in\mathrm{Dom}(v)}{\max}|\mathrm{Dom}(u)|

\displaystyle={\left\lfloor 2^{y^{*}_{u}}\right\rfloor}

\displaystyle\leq L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}\quad\text{ follows from~\eqref{eq:gen-dual-ub-2}}.

Based on this instance, we obtain a lower bound of

\displaystyle|\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}|

\displaystyle\geq\underset{u\in V}{\prod}|\mathrm{Dom}(u)|

\displaystyle=\underset{u\in V}{\prod}{\left\lfloor 2^{y^{*}_{u}}\right\rfloor}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2^{|V|}}\underset{u\in V}{\prod}2^{y^{*}_{u}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{|V|}}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\cdot L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}^{z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right),

where the first inequality follows by our definition of
|\mathrm{Dom}(u)|
and the final equality follows from strong duality. In particular, at optimality,
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
and its dual have the same objective value and since
(\mathbf{x}^{*},\mathbf{z}^{*})
and
\mathbf{y}^{*}
are optimal solutions to
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
and its dual respectively, this completes the proof.

A similar construction as above holds for any orientation of
G
even when only
\ell_{p}
-norm constraints are given (and no
\ell_{\infty}
constraints are given), leading to the following corollary.

Corollary F.1
.

For any
G
, any
p\in[1,\infty)
and an optimal solution
\mathbf{x}^{*}=(x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}
to
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
on
G
, there exists an instance
I=\{R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}:||R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}||_{p}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E\}
such that

\displaystyle|\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2^{|V|}}\cdot\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{x^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right).

Appendix G
Missing Details in Section
4.2

In this section, our goal is to prove (
10
). As discussed in Section
4.2
, we invoke Theorem
3.2
on
G
and consider the
G_{i}
s one-by-one – if
G_{i}
is a DAG, we can invoke Theorem
4.3
without any
\ell_{\infty}
constraints to prove (
10
). We fall back to the case when
G_{i}
contains at least one cycle
C
and that will be the main focus of this section. Since
|E|=|V|
, there are
|C|
edge disjoint (directed) trees each one rooted at a unique
A_{i}\in C
. Note that this is a standard graph theory result and we prove it in Appendix
G.4
.

Consider the topological ordering for each of the
|C|
edge (directed) disjoint trees denoted by
\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}}
for every
A_{i}\in C
. Let
\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}
denote an ordering
before
and
after

A_{i}
in
\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}}
. The following result is true.

Corollary G.1
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}=\left(\left(\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\right)_{A_{i}\in C},C,\left(\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}\right)_{A_{i}\in C}\right)

(70)

is a valid topological ordering for
G_{i}^{\prime}
, which is essentially
G_{i}
and we treat the cycle
C
as a single vertex.

For simplicity of notation, we will assume
G=G_{i}
for the rest of this argument. Since the
G_{i}
s are a disjoint union by Theorem
3.2
, we can apply the same argument on each
G_{i}
. We define some notation. Let
\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}
denote the set of vertices with incoming/outgoing edges to
A_{i}
for every
A_{i}\in C
and
L_{{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}}
denotes the
\ell_{p}
-norm constraint corresponding to
d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}
. We follow the same proof structure as in Section
4
and in particular, Section
4.1
.

We first state the corresponding version of Theorem
4.1
.

Corollary G.2
.

For any
G
with
|E|=|V|
and every
\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}(\mathbf{d})|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G)=\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}:A_{i}\in C}{\prod}\mathcal{D}_{u}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)\cdot\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left\{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)\cdot\prod_{A_{j}\in C,j\neq i}\mathcal{D}_{j}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)\right\}\cdot\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\prod}\mathcal{D}_{u}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)

(71)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\left\{d_{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})}\right\}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}},\left\{\frac{L_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathbf{d}})}^{p}}{d_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}})}^{p}}\right\}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},\frac{L_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}^{p}}{d_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}^{p}}\right\}\quad A_{i}\in C

(72)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{i}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)=\min\left\{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbf{d}),d_{({A_{i-1}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})}\right\}\quad A_{i}\in C

(73)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{u}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)=\min\left\{\{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E},\left\{\frac{L_{({u}\rightarrow{w},{\mathbf{d}})}^{p}}{d_{({u}\rightarrow{w})}^{p}}\right\}_{({u}\rightarrow{w})\in E}\right\}\quad u\in V\setminus V(C).

(74)

Further,
|\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}(\mathbf{d})|
can be computed in time
O(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G))
.

We would like to mention here that
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G)
in the claim above is different from the upper bound
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)
for the acyclic case. To prove Corollary (
G.2
), we consider all possible acyclic subgraphs (each one obtained by deleting a back edge from the cycle) and invoke Theorem
4.1
on each of these acyclic subgraphs. The upper bound follows as a result. From the algorithmic aspect, this translates to running Algorithm
2.2
over all these acyclic subgraphs and we can in fact, with the knowledge of
p
and the corresponding
\ell_{p}
-norm bound compute the ordering achieving the minimum as well.

Next, we restate our primal
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
for this scenario.

\displaystyle\min\quad\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\log(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})

(
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
)

\displaystyle\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}+\underset{({u}\rightarrow{w})\in E}{\sum}\frac{x_{({u}\rightarrow{w})}}{p}\geq 1\quad\forall u\in V

(75)

\displaystyle x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\geq 0\quad\forall({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E.

(76)

We are now ready to state the corresponding version of Lemma
4.2
.

Lemma G.3
.

For any
G
satisfying
|E|=|V|
with girth at least
p+1
, any feasible solution
\mathbf{x}=(x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}
to
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
on
G
and degree configuration
\mathbf{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G)\leq\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}},

(77)

where
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G)
is defined in (
71
).

We note here that our assumption of
G
having girth at least
p+1
in Theorem
4.4
stems from this result.
For computing
|\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}(\mathbf{d})|
, we first identify the
u\in V(C)
that achieves the
\min
in
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},C)
. Since
D^{\prime}_{u}
does not contain the incoming degree constraint
d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}
(where
({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(C)
), we can run Algorithm
2.2
on a topological ordering
(u,\dots,v)
of
C
and it would run in time
O(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G))
.

Finally, we can use these two results to prove (
10
) assuming Lemma
G.3
is true.

Proof of (
10
).

Invoking Lemma
G.3
and summing
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)
over all possible degree configurations and using the upper bound above, we have

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{d}=\left(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G)

\displaystyle\leq\underset{\mathbf{d}=\left(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}\quad\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(\underset{\mathbf{d}=\left(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}}{\sum}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}}

\displaystyle=\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}}=\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}.

Here, the second inequality follows by applying Hölder’s inequality assuming
\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}\geq 1
(which we argue below). The second equation follows from the fact that

\underset{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{\sum}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{p}=L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}^{p}

(by definition of
L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}
).

We argue
\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}\geq 1
as follows:

\displaystyle\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}\frac{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p+1}\underset{u\in V}{\sum}\left(\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}+\underset{({u}\rightarrow{w})\in E}{\sum}\frac{x_{({u}\rightarrow{w})}}{p}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{|V|}{p+1}\geq 1.

Here, the first inequality follows from (
75
) for every
u\in V
and the second inequality follows from
p\leq|V|-1
. Note that from the algorithm point of view (li.e., Algorithm
2.2
), to compute
J
, Algorithm
2.2
needs to go over all possible acyclic subgraphs in the cycle in
G
. The remaining runtime arguments follow through with additional linear factors in
|V|
and
|E|
.

This completes the proof.
∎

Our remaining work in this section is to prove Lemma
G.3
, which we do in two broad steps. We define
some notation:

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{In}}=\left\{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}}):A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}\in C\right\}

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{out}}=\left\{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}):A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{out}},A_{i}\in C\right\}

\displaystyle E_{R}=E\setminus\left(E(C)\cup E_{\mathrm{In}}\cup E_{\mathrm{out}}\right).

Through the rest of this section, when we use
(i+1)
and
(i-1)
, we mean
(i+1)\bmod k
and
(i-1)\bmod k
, where
k
is the length of the cycle in
G
.

G.1
Proof of Lemma
G.3

We start by modeling the computation of the logarithmic version of
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G)
as a linear program relaxation (which we call
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
) and defined below:

\displaystyle\max\quad\left(\underset{u\in V\setminus V(C)}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+z_{C}

(
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
)

\displaystyle\text{ s.t. }z_{C}\leq z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(78)

\displaystyle\frac{z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y_{A_{i+1}}^{\prime}\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(79)

\displaystyle\frac{y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}}{p}+z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})\in E_{\mathrm{In}}

(80)

\displaystyle\frac{z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}})\in E_{\mathrm{out}}

(81)

\displaystyle\frac{y_{v}}{p}+y_{u}\leq\log(L_{{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E_{R}

(82)

\displaystyle y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}\leq z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(83)

\displaystyle z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\geq 0\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(84)

\displaystyle z_{C},y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}\geq 0\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(85)

\displaystyle y_{u}\geq 0\quad\forall u\in V\setminus V(C).

(86)

We prove the following two results, which when combined together prove Lemma
G.3
.

Lemma G.4
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}}.

Lemma G.5
.

\displaystyle 2^{\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}}

\displaystyle\leq\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}},

where
\mathbf{x}=(x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}
is a feasible solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
on
G
with values
(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})})_{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}
.

The proof of Lemma
G.4
follows from standard linear programming techniques to convert
\min
bounds into linear programming relaxations and as a result, we defer it to Appendix
G.3
. We prove Lemma
G.5
here using the following claim (the proof is Section
G.1.2
).

Claim G.6
.

There exists an optimal solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
such that

\displaystyle\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right)=0.

(87)

G.1.1
Proof of Lemma
G.5

Our proof will use the dual of
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
(which we call
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
) and weak duality. We start by restating the dual here.

\displaystyle\max\quad\underset{u\in V}{\sum}y_{u}

\displaystyle\frac{y_{v}}{p}+y_{u}\leq\log(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\quad\forall({u}\rightarrow{v})\in E

(88)

\displaystyle y_{u}\geq 0\quad\forall u\in V

(89)

We will now restate the dual in a language closer to the notation in this section.

\displaystyle\max\quad\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}:A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+\left(\underset{u\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}:A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)

(
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
)

\displaystyle\frac{y_{A_{i}}}{p}+y_{A_{i+1}}\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(90)

\displaystyle\frac{y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}}{p}+y_{A_{i}}\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})\in E_{\mathrm{In}}

(91)

\displaystyle\frac{y_{A_{i}}}{p}+y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}})\in E_{\mathrm{out}}

(92)

\displaystyle\frac{y_{v}}{p}+y_{u}\leq\log(L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})})\quad\forall({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E_{R}

(93)

\displaystyle y_{u}\geq 0\quad\forall u\in V.

(94)

Here, we decompose the objective value based on Corollary
G.1
and we decompose the constraints based on definitions of
E_{\mathrm{In}},E_{\mathrm{out}}
and
E_{R}
respectively.

Proof of Lemma
G.5
.

We first argue that there exists an optimal solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
on
G
that can be converted to a feasible solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
on
G
. Since the objective values of both
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
and
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
are maximizing, this completes the proof.

Consider a feasible solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}=\left(\left(y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C},\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\right)_{A_{i}\in C},\left(y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right)_{A_{i}\in C},z_{C},\left(y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}\right)

We construct the following solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
, where we have (with a slight abuse of notation)

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}^{\prime}=\left(\left(y_{u}=y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C},\left(y_{A_{i}}=y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right)_{A_{i}\in C},\left(y_{u}=y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}\right)

With this assignment, note that the only difference between the linear programs
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
and
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
is that the set of variables
(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})})_{A_{i}\in C}
and
z_{C}
in
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
are not present in
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
.

Next, we argue that the solution
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
we constructed is feasible for
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
(i.e., satisfies constraints (
90
)–(
94
)). Recall from definition of
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}=\left\{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}}):A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}\in C\right\}

\displaystyle E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}=\left\{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}):A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{out}},A_{i}\in C\right\}

\displaystyle E_{R}=E\setminus\left(E(C)\cup E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\cup E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}\right).

We start with (
90
).

\displaystyle\frac{y_{A_{i}}}{p}+y_{A_{i+1}}\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall A_{i}\in C.

(95)

In particular, we have (by our construction) for every
A_{i}\in C
:

\displaystyle\frac{y_{A_{i}}}{p}+y_{A_{i+1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}}{p}+y_{A_{i+1}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y_{A_{i+1}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}}),

where the equality follows by definition, first inequality follows from (
83
) and the final inequality follows from (
79
). We can make similar arguments for (
91
). For edges in
E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}
and
E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}
, we can do a similar argument as above using (
81
), (
80
) and (
83
). Finally, for edges in
E_{R}
, we have (from (
82
)):

\displaystyle\frac{y_{v}}{p}+y_{u}\leq\log(L_{{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}}).

The remaining constraints in
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
are satisfied as they directly follow from (
85
) and (
86
) (of
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
). We restate them here for the sake of completeness.

\displaystyle y_{A_{i}}=y_{A}^{\prime}\geq 0\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

\displaystyle\left(y_{u}=y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}

\displaystyle\left(y_{u}=y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}.

To complete the proof, we argue that the optimal objective value of
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
is equal to the
objective value of
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
. We first claim that the following is true at optimally for
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
:

\displaystyle z_{C}=\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}\right).

(96)

Assuming this is true, we can take an optimal solution
(\mathbf{y}_{u},z_{c})
to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
that satisfies Claim
G.6
and rewrite the optimal objective value of
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
as follows:

\displaystyle\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+z_{C}+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}\right)+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+\left(\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}\right)+\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}\right)+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+\left(\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}\right)+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right),

where the final equation follows from Claim
G.6
. Note that this shows the optimal objective value of
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
is equal to the objective value of
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
. To complete this argument, we prove (
96
). Recall from (
78
) that
z_{C}
is upper bounded by
z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}
for every
A_{i}\in C
. In particular, this implies

\displaystyle z_{C}\leq\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}\right).

Recall that the objective value of
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
is maximizing and as a result, if
\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}\right)
is greater than
z_{C}
, we can always increase
z_{C}
to make it equal to
\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}\right)
. Note that this can only increase the objective value of
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
resulting solution is still feasible since
z_{C}
is not involved in any other constraint except
z_{C}\geq 0
.

This shows
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}\leq\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
and using strong duality, we have that the optimal objective values of both
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
and
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
are equal. This completes the proof, showing that

\displaystyle 2^{\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}}

\displaystyle=2^{\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}}

\displaystyle\leq\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}L_{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}^{x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}},

as required.
∎

G.1.2
Proof of Claim
G.6

At a high level, we show how to convert any optimal solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
to the form stated in this claim. We start by assuming that there exists an optimal solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}^{*}=\left(\left(y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C},\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\right)_{A_{i}\in C},\left(y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right)_{A_{i}\in C},z_{C},\left(y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}\right)=\epsilon>0.

(97)

Then, we construct a related solution

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}^{*}=\left(\left(y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C},\left(z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\right)_{A_{i}\in C},\left(y^{*}_{A_{i}}\right)_{A_{i}\in C},z_{C},\left(y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}\right)

to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
using
\mathbf{y}^{*}
, where we set (assuming
k=|C|
)

\displaystyle z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}=z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-\frac{p\cdot\epsilon}{k},\quad y^{*}_{A_{i}}=y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon}{k}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C.

(98)

Note that the remaining values
\left(\left(y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C},\left(y_{u}\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}\right)
remain the same. Note if we argue
\mathbf{z}^{*}
is an optimal solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
, then that completes the proof of Claim
G.6
. We argue this in two steps.

Lemma G.7
.

The objective value of
\mathbf{z}^{*}
is at least the optimal objective value of
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
.

Lemma G.8
.

\mathbf{z}^{*}
is a feasible solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
.

The latter proof follows from standard techniques and we defer it to Appendix
G.2
. We prove Lemma
G.7
here.

Proof of Lemma
G.7
.

Recall that
\mathbf{y}^{*}
is an optimal solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
with objective value

\displaystyle\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+z_{C}+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right).

and
\mathbf{z}^{*}
has objective value

\displaystyle\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y^{*}_{A_{i}}\right)+\left(\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y^{*}_{A_{i}}\right)+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right).

Our goal here is to prove that the objective value of
\mathbf{z}^{*}
is at least the objective value of
\mathbf{y}^{*}
. More formally, we prove

\displaystyle\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y^{*}_{A_{i}}\right)+\left(\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y^{*}_{A_{i}}\right)+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+z_{C}+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}\right)+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right)+\left(\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right)+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+\mathbf{\epsilon}+\left(\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right)+\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right),

(99)

where the first inequality follows from definition of
\mathbf{y}^{*}
and the second inequality follows from (
97
). Since the terms
\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)
and
\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\sum}y_{u}\right)
are common to both sides of the above expression, we can ignore them for the rest of the argument. We start by considering the left-hand side of (
99
):

\displaystyle\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y^{*}_{A_{i}}\right)+\left(\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y^{*}_{A_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-\frac{p\cdot\epsilon}{k}-y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}-\frac{\epsilon}{k}\right)+\sum_{A_{i}\in C}\left(y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon}{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y_{A_{i}}-\frac{(p+1)\epsilon}{k}\right)+\sum_{A_{i}\in C}\left(y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}+\frac{\epsilon}{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y_{A_{i}}\right)-\frac{p+1}{k}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon+\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{k-1-p}{k}\cdot\epsilon+\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y_{A_{i}}\right)+\left(\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{k-1-p}{k}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon+\left(\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\cdot k-1-p}{k}\cdot\epsilon+\left(\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon+\left(\sum_{A_{i}\in C}y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\right),\text{ since }p\leq k-1.

Here, the first equality follows by substituting the values of
z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}
and
y^{*}_{A_{i}}
(from (
98
)) for every
A_{i}\in C
and the first inequality follows by substituting
\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left(z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y_{A_{i}}\right)\geq\epsilon
(from (
97
)). This proves (
99
), as desired.
∎

G.2
Proof of Lemma
G.8

Proof of Lemma
G.8
.

To argue the feasibility of
\mathbf{z}^{*}
, we go over the constraints of
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
one-by-one and show how each one is satisfied. Throughout the proof, we will use the values
z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}
and
y^{*}_{A_{i}}
for every
A_{i}\in C
from (
98
).

We start by proving

\displaystyle z_{C}\leq z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y^{*}_{A_{j}}

below. We have

\displaystyle z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y^{*}_{A_{j}}

\displaystyle=z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-\frac{p\epsilon}{k}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}\left(y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon}{k}\right)\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{(k-1)\epsilon}{k}-\frac{p\epsilon}{k}\right)+z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

\displaystyle\geq z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

\displaystyle\geq z_{C}.

Here, the first equation follows by direct substitution. Further, the first inequality follows from our assumption that
p\leq k-1
and the second inequality follows from (
78
). This shows that
\mathbf{z}^{*}
satisfies (
78
).

Next, we argue

\displaystyle\frac{z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y^{*}_{A_{i+1}}\leq\log(L_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})})\quad\forall A_{i}\in C.

(100)

As in the earlier case, we substitute these values from (
98
)

\displaystyle\frac{z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y^{*}_{A_{i+1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-\frac{p\cdot\epsilon}{k}}{p}+y^{*}_{A_{i+1}}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

\displaystyle=\frac{z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}-\frac{\epsilon}{k}+y^{\prime}_{A_{i+1}}+\frac{\epsilon}{k}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

\displaystyle=\frac{z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y^{\prime}_{A_{i+1}}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

\displaystyle\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall A_{i}\in C,

where the final inequality follows from (
79
) and proves (
100
), as required. Recall from definition of
\mathrm{LP}^{(**)}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}=\left\{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}}):A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}\in C\right\}

\displaystyle E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}=\left\{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}):A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{out}},A_{i}\in C\right\}

\displaystyle E_{R}=E\setminus\left(E(C)\cup E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\cup E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}\right).

We can do a similar argument for arguing the following two inequalities as well:

\displaystyle\frac{z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}\quad\leq\quad\log\left(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathbf{d}})}}\right)\quad\forall({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}})\in E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}

\displaystyle\frac{y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}}{p}+z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\quad\leq\quad\log\left(L_{{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}}\right)\quad\forall({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})\in E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}},

which we argue below:

\displaystyle\frac{z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}-\frac{\epsilon}{k}+y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}

\displaystyle\leq\quad\frac{z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}

\displaystyle\leq\quad\log\left(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathbf{d}})}}\right)\quad\forall({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}})\in E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}

\displaystyle\frac{y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}}{p}+z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}

\displaystyle=\frac{y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}}{p}+z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-\frac{p\cdot\epsilon}{k}

\displaystyle\leq\quad\frac{y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}}{p}+z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}

\displaystyle\leq\quad\log\left(L_{{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}}\right)\quad\forall({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})\in E_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}.

Note that the final inequality in both constraints come from (
81
) and (
80
) respectively. For all the remaining edges (i.e., in
E_{R}
), we have (directly by definition)

\displaystyle\frac{y_{v}}{p}+y_{u}\leq\log\left(L_{{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}}\right)\quad\forall({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E_{R}.

Next, we show

\displaystyle y^{*}_{A_{i}}\leq z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C,

which we rewrite by substituting
z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}
and
y^{*}_{A_{i}}
for every
A_{i}\in C
as follows:

\displaystyle z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y^{*}_{A_{i}}

\displaystyle=z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-\frac{p\cdot\epsilon}{k}-\left(y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}+\frac{\epsilon}{k}\right)

\displaystyle=z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}-y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}-\frac{(p+1)\epsilon}{k}

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon-\frac{(p+1)\cdot\epsilon}{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon(k-1-p)}{k}

\displaystyle\geq 0.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle z^{*}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}

\displaystyle\geq y^{*}_{A_{i}}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

\displaystyle\geq y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon}{k}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

\displaystyle\geq 0,

where the final inequality follows from the fact that
y_{A_{i}}^{\prime},\epsilon\geq 0
. Note that this implies
y^{*}_{A_{i}}\geq 0
as well for every
A_{i}\in C
. Since
z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})},y^{\prime}_{A_{i}}\geq 0
for every
A_{i}\in C
, this implies
z_{C}\geq 0
. Further, we already have
y_{u}\geq 0\quad\forall u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}}\cup\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},\forall A_{i}\in C
(by definition). Thus,
\mathbf{z}^{*}
satisfies all constraints of
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
and is feasible, completing the proof.
∎

G.3
Proof of Lemma
G.4

We start by restating
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
below.

\displaystyle\max\quad\left(\underset{u\in V\setminus V(C)}{\sum}y_{u}\right)+z_{C}

(
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
)

\displaystyle\text{ s.t. }z_{C}\leq z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}y_{A_{j}}^{\prime}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(101)

\displaystyle\frac{z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y_{A_{i+1}}^{\prime}\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(102)

\displaystyle\frac{y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}}{p}+z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})\in E_{\mathrm{In}}

(103)

\displaystyle\frac{z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}}{p}+y_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}}\leq\log(L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}})\in E_{\mathrm{out}}

(104)

\displaystyle\frac{y_{v}}{p}+y_{u}\leq\log(L_{{({v}\rightarrow{u},{\mathbf{d}})}})\quad\forall({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E_{R}

(105)

\displaystyle y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}\leq z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(106)

\displaystyle z_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}\geq 0\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(107)

\displaystyle z_{C},y_{A_{i}}^{\prime}\geq 0\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

(108)

\displaystyle y_{u}\geq 0\quad\forall u\in V\setminus V(C).

(109)

Proof of Lemma
G.4
.

We start by showing that for every degree configuration

\mathbf{d}=\left(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)_{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq L_{{({v}\rightarrow{u})}},({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E},

there exists a feasible solution to
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
with objective value
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)
. Since
\mathcal{B}(G)
is the maximum among all degree configurations and
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
has a maximizing objective value, the proof immediately follows.

Recall from (
71
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},G)

\displaystyle=\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}}:A_{i}\in C}{\prod}\mathcal{D}_{u}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)\right)\cdot\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left\{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)\cdot\prod_{A_{j}\in C,j\neq i}\mathcal{D}_{j}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)\right\}\cdot\left(\underset{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}{\prod}\mathcal{D}_{u}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)\right),

where
\mathcal{D}_{i}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)
,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)
and
\mathcal{D}_{u}\left(\mathbf{d}\right)
are defined in (
73
), (
72
) for every
A_{i}\in C
and (
74
) for every
u\in V\setminus C
.

We now construct a feasible solution
\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}
for
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
with objective value
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)
, starting with some notation. We define
\widetilde{z}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}(\mathbf{d})
as

\displaystyle\min\left\{\left(\log\left(d_{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})}\right)\right)_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}},\left(\log\left(\frac{L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}}{d_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}})}^{p}}\right)\right)_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},\log\left(\frac{L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}}{d_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}^{p}}\right)\right\}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C,

(110)

followed by
\widetilde{y}^{\prime}_{A_{i}}(\mathbf{d})
(for every
A_{i}\in C
)

\displaystyle\min\left\{\left(\log\left\{d_{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})}\right\}\right)_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}},\left\{\log\left(\frac{L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}}{d_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}})}^{p}}\right)\right\}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},\log\left(d_{({A_{i-1}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})}\right),\log\left(\frac{L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}}{d_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}^{p}}\right)\right\}

(111)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{z}_{C}(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle=\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left\{\widetilde{z}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}(\mathbf{d})+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}\widetilde{y}^{\prime}_{A_{j}}(\mathbf{d})\right\}

(112)

\displaystyle\widetilde{y}_{u}(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\left\{\log\left(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)\right\}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E},\left\{\frac{L_{{({u}\rightarrow{w},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}}{d_{({u}\rightarrow{w})}^{p}}\right\}_{({u}\rightarrow{w})\in E}\right\}\quad\forall u\in V\setminus C.

(113)

We are now ready to define
\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}=\left(\left(\widetilde{y}_{u}(\mathbf{d})\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}},A_{i}},A_{i}\in C},\left(\widetilde{z}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}(\mathbf{d})\right)_{A_{i}\in C},\left(\widetilde{y}^{\prime}_{A_{i}}(\mathbf{d})\right)_{A_{i}\in C},\widetilde{z}_{C}(\mathbf{d}),\left(\widetilde{y}_{u}(\mathbf{d})\right)_{u\in\mathcal{T}_{A_{i},A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},A_{i}\in C}\right)

Next, we show that
\bar{y}
satisfies all constraints in
\mathrm{LP}^{(*)}
.

We start with (

):

\displaystyle\widetilde{z}_{C}(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle=\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\min}\left\{\widetilde{z}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}(\mathbf{d})+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}\widetilde{y}^{\prime}_{A_{j}}(\mathbf{d})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{z}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}(\mathbf{d})+\sum_{A_{j}\in C\setminus A_{i}}\widetilde{y}_{A_{j}}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d})\quad\forall A_{i}\in C,

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{z}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}(\mathbf{d})}{p}+\widetilde{y}^{\prime}_{A_{i}}(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle=\frac{\min\left\{\left\{\log\left(d_{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})}\right)\right\}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}},\left\{\log\left(\frac{L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}}{d_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}})}^{p}}\right)\right\}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},\log\left(\frac{L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}}{d_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}^{p}}\right)\right\}}{p}

\displaystyle+\min\left\{\left\{\log\left(d_{({A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})}\right)\right\}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}},\left\{\log\left(\frac{L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}}{d_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}})}^{p}}\right)\right\}_{A_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}},\log\left(d_{({A_{i-1}}\rightarrow{A_{i}})}\right),\log\left(\frac{L_{{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}}^{p}}{d_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}^{p}}\right)\right\}\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

\displaystyle=\log\left(L_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\quad\forall A_{i}\in C,

where the first equation follows by direct substitution and the inequality follows from the definition of
\min
.

\displaystyle\widetilde{z}_{({A_{i}}\rightarrow{A_{i+1}})}(\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{y}_{A_{i}}^{\prime}(\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{z}_{C}\geq 0\quad\forall A_{i}\in C

G.4
Proof of Corollary
G.1

The proof of Corollary
G.1
follows directly from the proof of the claim below.

Claim G.9
.

Let
G
be a connected graph (in the undirected sense) with a directed cycle
C
such that
|E|=|V|
. Consider the graph without edges in the cycle
C
i.e.,
G^{\prime}=(V(G),E(G)\setminus E(C))
and for every vertex
A_{i}\in C
, let
\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{In}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}
denote the set of incoming and outgoing edges from
A_{i}
that are not in
E(C)
. Then,
G^{\prime}
has
|C|
edge disjoint trees with each containing a unique node
A_{i}
in
C
.

Proof of Claim
G.9
.

We prove this claim in two steps – first, we argue that the graph
G^{\prime}=(V(G),E(G)\setminus E(C))
is a forest with
|C|
-edge disjoint trees. Then, we argue that all these trees contain an unique node in
C
.

The proof is by contradiction. Assume that the
|C|
trees are not edge disjoint i.e., there exists at least one edge
({v}\rightarrow{u})
between these trees. We now argue that this would imply
|E(G)|>|V(G)|
and start by counting the number of edges in
G^{\prime}
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle|E(G^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=1+\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\sum}\left(|\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}}|-1\right)

\displaystyle=1-|C|+\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\sum}|\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}}|

\displaystyle=1-|C|+|V|,

where the last equation follows from the fact that
\underset{A_{i}\in C}{\cup}|\mathcal{T}_{A_{i}}|=|V|
. Note that this implies
|E|=|E(G^{\prime})|+|E(C)|=|V|-|C|+1+|C|=|V|+1
, which contradicts our earlier assumption that
|E|=|V|
.

To complete the proof, we argue that each of these trees contains an unique node in
C
. Note that if otherwise i.e., the tree contains no nodes from
C
, then that implies the original graph
G
was not connected in the undirected sense.
∎

We construct our lower bound instance for

L^{\prime}

and

32
32

32

We would like note there that in our arguments in the section, we use the undirected edge
({v},{u})
instead of the standard directed version
({v}\rightarrow{u})
used in the rest of the paper. Note that this doesn’t break correctness since for

-norm bounds, the direction does not matter and we (implicitly) assume that all tuples in the relation

are directed from

For each

\displaystyle\left\{[(j-1)\cdot d^{\prime}+1,j\cdot d^{\prime}]\times[(j-1)\cdot d^{\prime}+1,j\cdot d^{\prime}]:j\in\left[\frac{L^{\prime}}{(d^{\prime})^{2}}\right]\right\}.

(116)

Further, for every
({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E:s_{1}\in S_{1}(G),t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)
, we define

\displaystyle R_{({s_{1}},{t_{1}})}

\displaystyle=\left\{\left[\frac{L^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}\right]\times[d^{\prime}]\right\}

(117)

and for every edge

and

\displaystyle R_{({s_{2}},{t_{2}})}

\displaystyle=\{[L^{\prime}]\times[1]\}.

(118)

All nodes
u
that have not been assigned a domain yet get
\mathrm{Dom}(u)=[d^{\prime}]
and all the unasigned relations are set as

\displaystyle R_{({v},{u})}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Dom}(v)\times\mathrm{Dom}(u).

(119)

We make the following assumption based on our construction above:

\displaystyle||R_{({v},{u})}||_{1}

\displaystyle\leq L\quad\forall({v},{u})\in E

(120)

\displaystyle||R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}||_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq d\quad\forall({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E.

(121)

Assuming the above is true, we argue our lower bound. For each
C_{i}\in C(G)
, we have a lower bound of

\displaystyle\frac{L^{\prime}}{(d^{\prime})^{2}}\underset{u\in V(C_{i})}{\prod}d^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{L^{\prime}}{(d^{\prime})^{2}}(d^{\prime})^{|C_{i}|}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2^{|C_{i}|}}Ld^{|C_{i}|-2}

on the size of the join projected down to relations in
C_{i}
. Further, note that these bounds are ‘independent’ for each

and once we fix the projection of output tuples in

, the rest is just a Cartesian product of the domain size for each vertex (which are

and

for vertices in

and

respectively). Combining these bounds together, we get the desired lower bound of

as required.

To complete the proof , we still need to argue (
120
) and (
121
). For each
C_{i}\in C(G)
, we have (by definition) for every

\displaystyle||R_{({v},{u})}||_{1}

\displaystyle=\underset{j\in\left[\frac{L^{\prime}}{(d^{\prime})^{2}}\right]}{\sum}(d^{\prime})^{2}\leq L

\displaystyle||R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}||_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\underset{j\in\left[\frac{L^{\prime}}{(d^{\prime})^{2}}\right]}{\max}d^{\prime}\leq d.

Further, for every
({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E:s_{1}\in S_{1}(G),t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)

and for every edge
({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E:s_{2}\in S_{2}(G),t_{2}\in T_{2}(G)

\displaystyle||R_{({s_{2}},{t_{2}})}||_{1}\leq L,\quad||R_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}||_{\infty}=1\leq d.

Next, we reason about the crossing edges. For edges
({v}\rightarrow{t_{1}})
between
C_{i}
(for some

\displaystyle=\underset{j\in\left[\frac{L^{\prime}}{(d^{\prime})^{2}}\right]}{\sum}d^{\prime}=\frac{L^{\prime}}{d^{\prime}}\leq L

\displaystyle||R_{({v}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}||_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\underset{j\in\left[\frac{L^{\prime}}{(d^{\prime})^{2}}\right]}{\max}d^{\prime}\leq d

and

Finally, for each remaining edge, we have

\displaystyle||R_{({v},{u})}||_{1}=d^{\prime 2}\leq d^{2}\leq L,\quad||R_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}||_{\infty}=\underset{j\in[d^{\prime}]}{\max}d^{\prime}\leq d.

This completes the proof.

H.1.2
Proof of (
12
)

We start by recalling the notation we defined for this section. Recall that each edge
({v},{u})
has
\ell_{\infty}
-norm constraints of the form
L_{(v\rightarrow u,\infty)}
and as a result, we consider
G
to be the directed graph on each
({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E
. Now, we decompose vertices in

into four buckets –
(1)
Set of non-trivial source Strongly Connected Components (SCCs) (i.e., source SCCs with at least two vertices)
C(G)
,
(2)
The remaining sources (source SCCs with one vertex)
S(G)
,
(3)
Set of vertices
T(G)
, where each vertex is connected by at least one vertex in
S(G)
(through an incoming edge) and
(4)
The remaining set of vertices
\rho(G)
. We consider the induced subgraph on vertices
S(G)\cup T(G)
, which we use to further decompose it into induced subgraphs
(S_{1}(G),T_{1}(G))
and
(S_{2}(G),T_{2}(G))
respectively.

We consider the induced subgraph on vertices
S(G)\cup T(G)
. We further partition
S
into
S_{1}(G)
and
S_{2}(G)
and
T
into
T_{1}(G)
and
T_{2}(G)
as follows. We choose a subset
E(S_{1}(G),T_{1}(G))\subset E(G)
to be a (disjoint) set of stars
33
33

33

A star with
n
vertices is where one vertex has degree
n-1
(which we call the
center
) and the remaining vertices have degree
1
(which we call
leaves
).
with each
s_{1}\in S_{1}
as the center (in the undirected sense) and each
t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)
s.t.
({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1}(G),T_{1}(G))
(for the fixed
s_{1}
) as a leaf. Similarly, we define
E(S_{2},T_{2})\subset E
to be another (disjoint) set of disjoint stars such with each
t_{2}\in T(G_{2})
is a center (in the undirected sense) and each
s_{2}\in S_{2}(G)
with
({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2}(G),T_{2}(G))
(for the fixed
t_{2}
) as a leaf. We pick
S_{i}(G),T_{i}(G)
and
E(S_{i},T_{i})
for
i\in[2]
that
minimizes
the size of this star cover, i.e. minimizes
\left|{E(S_{1},T_{1})}\right|+\left|{E(S_{2},T_{2})}\right|=\left|{T_{1}(G)}\right|+\left|{S_{2}(G)}\right|
. We show in the section below as to how to compute an optimal star cover of this kind using the AGM LP and also argue why minimizing the star cover size also minimizes our bound in Theorem
5.2
.

For each
C_{i}\in C(G)
, we fix an arbitrary edge
({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}})\in E(C_{i})
and drop all incoming/outgoing edges from
v_{i}
and all incoming edges from
u_{i}
. Note that each
C_{i}
has a single source vertex. For non-source non-trivial SCCs, we can drop the minimal subset of edges to make them acyclic. For each
C_{i}
, we will be treating the edge
({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}})
differently compared to the other ones. Since we are working with a spanning subgraph of
G
to prove our upper bound (
114
), note that we are answering Question
1.4
in affirmative in this process, in this setting.

We are now ready to restate (
12
) here and we would like to prove
|\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}|
is at most

\displaystyle 2^{2(|V|+|C(G)|+|S_{1}(G)|+|T_{1}(G)|)}\left(\underset{C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}Ld^{|C_{i}|-2}\right)\cdot\left(\left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^{|S_{1}(G)|}\cdot d^{|T_{1}(G)|}\right)\cdot L^{|S_{2}(G)|}\cdot d^{|\rho(G)|}.

Invoking Theorem
4.1
on
G
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\underset{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}}):C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}\frac{2\cdot L_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}}{d_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}})}}d_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}})}\cdot\underset{u\in V(C_{i})\setminus\{v_{i},u_{i}\}}{\prod}\quad\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(C_{i})}{\min}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{s_{1}\in S_{1}(G)}{\prod}\underset{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})}{\min}\frac{2\cdot L_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}}{d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}},{\mathbf{d}})}}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)}{\prod}\underset{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})}{\min}d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{s_{2}\in S_{2}(G)}{\prod}\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\min}\frac{2\cdot L_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})}}{d_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{t_{2}\in T_{2}(G)}{\prod}\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\min}d_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\left(\underset{u\in\rho(G)}{\prod}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\min}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{|C(G)|+|S_{1}(G)|+|S_{2}(G)|}\cdot\left(\underset{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}}):C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}L_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}\cdot\underset{u\in V(C_{i})\setminus\{v_{i},u_{i}\}}{\prod}\sqrt[|\mathrm{In}(u)|]{\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(C_{i})}{\prod}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{s_{1}\in S_{1}(G)}{\prod}\frac{L_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{s_{1}}},{\mathbf{d}})}}{d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{s_{1}}})}}\cdot d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{s_{1}}})}\cdot\underset{t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)\setminus t_{s_{1}}}{\prod}\sqrt[|\mathrm{In}(t_{1})|]{\underset{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})}{\prod}d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{s_{2}\in S_{2}(G)}{\prod}\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\min}\frac{L_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})}}{d_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}}\underset{t_{2}\in T_{2}(G)}{\prod}\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\min}d_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{u\in\rho(G)}{\prod}\sqrt[\mathrm{In}(u)|]{\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{|C(G)|+|S_{1}(G)|+|S_{2}(G)|}\cdot\left(\underset{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}}):C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}L_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}\cdot\underset{u\in V(C_{i})\setminus\{v_{i},u_{i}\}}{\prod}\sqrt[|\mathrm{In}(u)|]{\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(C_{i})}{\prod}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{s_{1}\in S_{1}(G)}{\prod}L_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{s_{1}}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)\setminus\{t_{s_{1}}:s_{1}\in S_{1}(G)\}}{\prod}\sqrt[\mathrm{In}(t_{1})|]{\underset{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})}{\prod}d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in(S_{2}(G),T_{2}(G))}{\prod}L_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{u\in\rho(G)}{\prod}\sqrt[\mathrm{In}(u)|]{\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right).

Here, the second inequality follows from the fact
|\mathrm{In}(u)|\geq 1
for every
u\in V\setminus(\underset{C_{i}\in C(G)}{\cup}\{v_{i}\}\cup S_{1}(G)\cup S_{2}(G))
(note that all these sets of vertices are source vertices by definition and our contruction). For each
s_{1}\in S_{1}(G)
, note that there is at least one vertex
t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)
such that
({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})
(by definition of the latter). We define
T_{S_{1}(G)}=\{t_{s_{1}}:s_{1}\in S(G),({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})\}
. It follows that
|T_{S_{1}}(G)|=|S_{1}(G)|
.

Summing up
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)
over all possible degree configurations
\mathbf{d}
, we get

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(G)}}{\sum}\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{d},G)

\displaystyle\leq\underset{\mathbf{d}=(d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(G)}}{\sum}2^{|C(G)|+|S_{1}(G)|+|S_{2}(G)|}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}}):C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}L_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}\cdot\underset{u\in V(C_{i})\setminus\{v_{i},u_{i}\}}{\prod}\sqrt[|\mathrm{In}(u)|]{\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(C_{i})}{\prod}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\left(\underset{s_{1}\in S_{1}(G)}{\prod}L_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{s_{1}}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)\setminus T_{S_{1}(G)}}{\prod}\sqrt[\mathrm{In}(t_{1})|]{\underset{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})}{\prod}d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\prod}L_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{u\in\rho(G)}{\prod}\sqrt[\mathrm{In}(u)|]{\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{|C(G)|+|S_{1}(G)|+|S_{2}(G)|}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}}):C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}\left(\underset{d_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}})}\leq L_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}}{\sum}L_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\cdot\underset{u\in V(C_{i})\setminus\{v_{i},u_{i}\}}{\prod}\quad\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(C_{i})}{\prod}\left(\underset{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq d}{\sum}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)^{\frac{1}{|\mathrm{In}(u)|}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}\leq L:({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})}{\sum}\quad\underset{s_{1}\in S_{1}(G)}{\prod}L_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{s_{1}}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)\setminus T_{S_{1}}(G)}{\prod}\quad\underset{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})}{\prod}\left(\underset{d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}\leq d}{\sum}d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}\right)^{\frac{1}{|\mathrm{In}(t_{1})|}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{d_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}\leq L:({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\sum}\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\prod}L_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{u\in\rho(G)}{\prod}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}\left(\underset{d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\leq d}{\sum}d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)^{\frac{1}{|\mathrm{In}(u)|}}\right)

(122)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{|C(G)|+|S_{1}(G)|+|S_{2}(G)|}\cdot\left(\underset{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}}):C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}L_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}})}\cdot\left(\underset{u\in V(C_{i})\setminus\{v_{i},u_{i}\}}{\prod}\quad\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(C_{i})}{\prod}(2d)^{\frac{1}{|\mathrm{In}(u)|}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}\leq L:({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})}{\sum}\quad\underset{s_{1}\in S_{1}(G)}{\prod}L_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)\setminus T_{S}(G)}{\prod}\quad\underset{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})}{\prod}(2d)^{\frac{1}{|\mathrm{In}(t_{1})|}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{d_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}\leq L:({s_{2}},{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\sum}\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\prod}L_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{u\in\rho(G)}{\prod}\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\prod}(2d)^{\frac{1}{|\mathrm{In}(u)|}}\right)

(123)

\displaystyle=2^{|C(G)|+|S_{1}(G)|+|S_{2}(G)|}\cdot\left(\underset{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}}):C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}L_{({v_{i}}\rightarrow{u_{i}})}\cdot\left(\underset{u\in V(C_{i})\setminus\{v_{i},u_{i}\}}{\prod}2d\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{s_{1}\in S_{1}(G)}{\prod}\underset{d_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}\leq L:({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})\in E(S_{1},T_{1})}{\sum}L_{({s_{1}}\rightarrow{t_{1}})}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{t_{1}\in T_{1}(G)}{\prod}2d\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\quad\left(\underset{d_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}\leq L:({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\sum}\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\prod}L_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\cdot\left(\underset{u\in\rho(G)}{\prod}2d\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{|V|+|C(G)|+|S_{1}(G)|+|S_{2}(G)|}\left(\underset{C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}Ld^{|C_{i}|-2}\right)\cdot\left(L^{|S_{1}(G)|}\cdot d^{|T_{1}(G)|-|T_{S_{1}(G)}|}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{d_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}\leq L:({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\sum}\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\prod}L_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\cdot d^{|\rho(G)|}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{|V|+|C(G)|+|S_{1}(G)|+|S_{2}(G)|}\left(\underset{C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}Ld^{|C_{i}|-2}\right)\cdot\left(L^{|S_{1}(G)|}d^{|T_{1}(G)|-|T_{S_{1}}(G)|}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\underset{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})\in E(S_{2},T_{2})}{\prod}\underset{d_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}\leq L}{\sum}L_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})}\right)d^{\rho(G)}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2(|V|+|C(G)|+|S_{1}(G)|+|S_{2}(G)|)}\left(\underset{C_{i}\in C(G)}{\prod}Ld^{|C_{i}|-2}\right)\cdot L^{|S_{1}(G)|}d^{|T_{1}(G)|-|S_{1}(G)|}\cdot L^{|S_{2}(G)|}\cdot d^{\rho(G)}.

(124)

Here (
122
) follows by a direct application of Hölder’s inequality using the fact that
|\mathrm{In}(u)|\geq 1
for every non-source vertex
u\in V
. As a result, we can push the sums in and then, (
123
) follows from the definition of
d_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}
s as powers of two and the sum from
1
to
d
is at most
2d
. Finally, (
124
) follows by applying
\underset{d_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}})}\leq L}{\sum}L_{({s_{2}}\rightarrow{t_{2}},{\mathbf{d}})}=L
and noting that
|T_{1}(G)|-|T_{S_{1}(G)}|=|T_{1}(G)|-|S_{1}(G)|
. This completes the proof.

H.1.3
Mapping Edge Cover of our Decomposition to Optimal Fractional Edge Cover

We first restate the fractional covering linear program for (the undirected version) of
E(S,T)
and with a slight abuse of notation assume
E(S,T)
to be containing the undirected set.

\displaystyle\min\quad\underset{e\in E(S,T)}{\sum}x_{e}

((
\mathrm{LP}^{\prime}
))

\displaystyle\underset{e\ni v}{\sum}x_{e}\geq 1\quad\forall v\in V

\displaystyle x_{e}\geq 0\quad\forall e\in E.

We state a well-known result based on the LP above (where we use the fact that
E(S,T)
is bipartite):

Theorem H.2

(Implicit in
[
22
]
)
.

There exists an optimal solution for
\mathrm{LP}^{\prime}
on
(S,T)
that can decomposed into a union of disjoint stars, where
x_{e}=\{0,1\}
for every
e\in E(S,T)
. Further, the set of edges
e
with
x_{e}=1
forms the disjoint set of stars.

We then pick
E(S_{1},T_{2})
and
E(S_{2},T_{2})
from the stars above. The following corollary holds.

Corollary H.3
.

For each edge
({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(S_{1},T_{2}),E(S_{2},T_{2})
, we have
x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}=1
.

This implies
E(S_{1},T_{2})
and
E(S_{2},T_{2})
are minimum integral edge coverings for
S,T
.

To complete this section, we argue that the min star cover size of
(S,T)
is the same as the min bound achieved by our upper bound in Theorem
5.2
. For this, we start by restating the portion of upper bound for the
(S,T)
part.

\displaystyle\left(\left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^{|S_{1}(G)|}\cdot d^{|T_{1}(G)|}\right)\cdot L^{|S_{2}(G)|}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^{\left|{S_{1}(G)}\right|+\left|{S_{2}(G)}\right|}\cdot d^{\left|{T_{1}(G)}\right|+\left|{S_{2}(G)}\right|}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^{\left|{S(G)}\right|}\cdot d^{\left|{T_{1}(G)}\right|+\left|{S_{2}(G)}\right|}.

In our upper bound,
\left|{S(G)}\right|
is fixed so miniming the above part is the same as minimizing
\left|{T_{1}(G)}\right|+\left|{s_{2}(G)}\right|
, which is exactly minimizing the size of star/integral edge cover, as desired.

H.2
Missing Details in Section
5.2

In this section, our goal is to prove
14
.

H.2.1
Notation and Existing Results

We restate
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
for this scenario, where
x_{({v},{u})}
corresponds to the
\ell_{1}
-norm bounds
34
34

34

Note here that we use
({v},{u})
here instead of the standard
x_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}
. We would like to note here that this is fine since for
\ell_{1}
-norm bounds, the direction does not matter and we (implicitly) assume that all tuples in the relation
R_{({v},{u})}
are directed from
v
to
u
.
and
z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}
correspond to the
\ell_{\infty}
-norm bounds.

\displaystyle\min\left(\underset{(v,u)\in E}{\sum}x_{({v},{u})}\log(L)+\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\log(d)\right)

(
\mathrm{LP}^{+}
)

\displaystyle\text{ s.t. }\left(\underset{e=(v,u)\ni u}{\sum}x_{v,u}\right)+\left(\underset{({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}{\sum}z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)\geq 1\quad\forall u\in V

(125)

\displaystyle x_{({v},{u})},z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\geq 0\quad\forall({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E.

We will be using a result similar to Theorem
3.2
on
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
(the proof is in Appendix
D.3
).

Corollary H.4
.

For any
G=(V,E)
, there exists an optimal basic feasible solution
(\mathbf{x}^{*},\mathbf{z}^{*})=(x^{*}_{({v},{u})},z^{*}_{({v}\rightarrow{u})})_{({v},{u}),({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E}
to
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
on
G
that can be decomposed into a disjoint union of
t
(some
t>0
) connected components (in the undirected sense)
G_{i}=(V_{i},E_{i})
with

\displaystyle|V_{i}|-1\leq|Q(E(G_{i}))|\leq|V_{i}|,\text{ where }

Q(E(G_{i}))
is the set of non-zero values
x_{({v},{u})}
and
z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}
for every
({v},{u})
and
({v}\rightarrow{u})
in
E(G_{i})
. Further,

(\mathbf{x}^{*}_{i},\mathbf{z}^{*}_{i})=\left(x_{({v},{u})},z_{({v}\rightarrow{u})}\right)_{({v},{u}),({v}\rightarrow{u})\in E(G_{i})}

is an optimal basic feasible solution for
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
on
G_{i}
for every
i\in[t]
and
\cup_{i=1}^{t}V(G_{i})=V
and
V(G_{i})\cap V(G_{j})=\emptyset

\forall i,j\in[t],i\neq j
. The following is true –
\mathrm{J_{G}^{(I)}}=\times_{i\in[t]}J_{G_{i}}^{(I)}
.

For our arguments, in addition to the above, we need an extremal property (Property

) as well, where consider a specific subclass of optimal basic feasible solutions to
\mathrm{LP}^{(+)}
on
